intervalIntegral_fourierCos_eq lemma source
lemma intervalIntegral_fourierCos_eq (ρ : Measure ℝ) [IsFiniteMeasure ρ] (hsym : IsSymmetric ρ)
    {r : ℝ} (hr : 0 < r) :
    ∫ ω in (-r)..r, fourierCos ρ ω = 2 * r * ∫ x, Real.sinc (r * x) ∂ρ

The transform of a symmetric measure, integrated over a symmetric interval, is what integral_charFun_Icc computes: ∫_{-r}^{r} ρ̂ = 2r ∫ sinc(r x) dρ, read on the real line.

Claimed by no released node.

integral_one_sub_sinc_le theorem source
theorem integral_one_sub_sinc_le (ρ : Measure ℝ) [IsProbabilityMeasure ρ] (hsym : IsSymmetric ρ)
    (hpos : ∀ ω, 0 < fourierCos ρ ω) {r : ℝ} (hr : 0 < r) :
    ∫ x, (1 - Real.sinc (r * x)) ∂ρ ≤ (2 * r)⁻¹ * ∫ ω in (-r)..r, exponent ρ ω

The truncation inequality, for a single symmetric kernel: the sinc defect is bounded by the mean of the exponent over [-r,r].

This is eq:truncation before the sum over the partition. The Fubini step is Mathlib's integral_charFun_Icc; what is added here is the passage from 1 - ρ̂ to g_ρ, which is log u ≤ u - 1.

Claimed by no released node.

fourierCos def source
noncomputable def fourierCos (μ : Measure ℝ) (ω : ℝ) : ℝ

The Fourier transform of a symmetric measure: μ̂(ω) = ∫ cos(ωx) μ(dx).

A Bochner integral of a bounded continuous function; for a finite μ it always converges, and for a symmetric μ it is the Fourier transform.

Claimed by no released node.

Unconditional, hence junk where μ̂ ≤ 0; every statement that reads exponent as -log of a positive number says so in its hypotheses.

fourierCos_eq_charFun_re theorem source
theorem fourierCos_eq_charFun_re (μ : Measure ℝ) [IsFiniteMeasure μ] (ω : ℝ) :
    fourierCos μ ω = (charFun μ ω).re

The cosine transform is the real part of the characteristic function.

fourierCos μ ω = ∫ cos(ωx) ∂μ and charFun μ ω = ∫ exp(ωx i) ∂μ, and re commutes with the Bochner integral of an integrable function.

Claimed by no released node.

charFun_eq_fourierCos_of_symmetric theorem source
theorem charFun_eq_fourierCos_of_symmetric {μ : Measure ℝ} [IsFiniteMeasure μ]
    (hsym : IsSymmetric μ) (ω : ℝ) :
    charFun μ ω = (fourierCos μ ω : ℂ)

For a symmetric finite measure the characteristic function is real, and equal to the cosine transform; so the sign convention is immaterial wherever a symmetric measure's transform is read.

The imaginary part is ∫ sin(ωx) ∂μ, which vanishes because μ is invariant under x ↦ -x and sin is odd.

1 - cos(ωv) ≤ (2 + ω²)(1 ∧ v²): the truncation both regimes of the cosine obey.

integrable_cos_mul theorem source

The cosine transform's integrand is integrable against a finite measure: it is continuous and bounded by 1.

Wave 1 of the proving campaign (2026-09-09) moved this here. It was proved three times independently — as integrable_cos in Paper V's SpatialLine/LatticeZero.lean, and as integrable_cos_mul in Nonvanishing and Pairing. TransformUniqueness

fourier_uniqueness theorem source
theorem fourier_uniqueness {μ ρ : Measure ℝ} [IsFiniteMeasure μ] [IsFiniteMeasure ρ]
    (h : ∀ ω, charFun μ ω = charFun ρ ω) : μ = ρ

prop:fourier-uniqueness. A finite Borel measure on ℝ is determined by its Fourier transform.

Stated for Mathlib's charFun, whose sign convention is the opposite of the blueprint's (2.1); the statement is symmetric in the convention, since ω ↦ -ω is a bijection of ℝ.

integral_fourierCos_mul_gaussTest theorem source
theorem integral_fourierCos_mul_gaussTest (ν : Measure ℝ) [IsFiniteMeasure ν] :
    ∫ ω, fourierCos ν ω * gaussTest ω = ∫ x, Real.exp (-(x ^ 2) / 2) ∂ν

The two forms of Θ agree. The frequency-side integral against the standard Gaussian density equals the measure-side integral of e^{-x²/2}.

Claimed by no released node.

strictAntiOn_transmittance theorem source
theorem strictAntiOn_transmittance (hker : IsKernelFamily Fam.Φ μ) (hnd : IsNondegenerate Fam.Φ) :
    StrictAntiOn (fun t => ∫ x, Real.exp (-(x ^ 2) / 2) ∂(μ 0 t)) (Ici 0)

Θ is strictly decreasing under (ND).

The integrand (1 - μ̂_{a,b}) μ̂_{0,a} ρ is nonnegative and vanishes exactly where μ̂_{a,b} = 1; the complement of that set is open and, by (ND) and the uniqueness of the transform, nonempty, hence of positive Lebesgue measure.

Claimed by no released node.

Transport

mconv_reflect theorem source
theorem mconv_reflect (hμ : IsSymmetric μ) {f : ℝ → ℝ} (hf : StronglyMeasurable f) (x : ℝ) :
    mconv μ (fun u => f (-u)) x = mconv μ f (-x)

(A3) at the level of functions: for a symmetric measure, convolution commutes with reflection. The change of variables is y ↦ -y, which IsSymmetric says leaves μ alone.

Claimed by no released node.

theorem mconvL1_reflL1 [IsFiniteMeasure μ] (hμ : IsSymmetric μ) (f : X) :
    mconvL1 μ (reflL1 f) = reflL1 (mconvL1 μ f)

(A3) for mconvL1: a symmetric measure gives an operator commuting with reflection.

dilate_mconv theorem source
theorem dilate_mconv {lam : ℝ} (hlam : 0 < lam) (μ : Measure ℝ) {f : ℝ → ℝ} (hf : Measurable f) :
    dilate lam (mconv μ f) = mconv (μ.map (fun x => lam * x)) (dilate lam f)

(A8) at the level of functions: dilating intertwines with dilating the measure.

dilL1_comp_mconvL1 theorem source
theorem dilL1_comp_mconvL1 {lam : ℝ} (hlam : 0 < lam) (μ : Measure ℝ) [IsFiniteMeasure μ]
    [IsFiniteMeasure (μ.map (fun x => lam * x))] :
    (dilL1 hlam).comp (mconvL1 μ) = (mconvL1 (μ.map (fun x => lam * x))).comp (dilL1 hlam)

(A8) for mconvL1: dilating intertwines with dilating the measure.

Claimed by no released node.

eq_dirac_of_mconvL1_eq_id theorem source
theorem eq_dirac_of_mconvL1_eq_id {μ : Measure ℝ} [IsProbabilityMeasure μ]
    (h : mconvL1 μ = ContinuousLinearMap.id ℝ X) : μ = Measure.dirac 0

The kernel of the identity is δ₀.

If convolution by a probability measure is the identity on L¹, the measure is δ₀. The contrapositive says that a kernel other than δ₀ gives an operator different from the identity, which is how the nondegeneracy clause (ND) of a concrete family is checked.

Claimed by no released node.

norm_sub_eq_lintegral theorem source
theorem norm_sub_eq_lintegral (a b : X) :
    ‖a - b‖ = (∫⁻ x, ‖(a : ℝ → ℝ) x - (b : ℝ → ℝ) x‖ₑ).toReal

The L¹ norm of a difference as a lower integral — the form the (A7) estimates of ScaleSpaceCore.L1Continuity speak in.

Truncation

one_sub_exp_neg_le theorem source
theorem one_sub_exp_neg_le (u : ℝ) : 1 - Real.exp (-u) ≤ u

0 ≤ u - (1 - e^{-u}) for u ≥ 0.

Claimed by no released node.

sub_one_sub_exp_neg_le theorem source
theorem sub_one_sub_exp_neg_le (u : ℝ) (hu : 0 ≤ u) :
    u - (1 - Real.exp (-u)) ≤ u ^ 2

u - (1 - e^{-u}) ≤ u² for u ≥ 0. The blueprint's u²/2 is sharp and needs a derivative argument; this constant follows from e^v ≥ 1 + v alone and is all the step uses.

Claimed by no released node.

sub_sin_eq_intervalIntegral lemma source
lemma sub_sin_eq_intervalIntegral (u : ℝ) :
    u - Real.sin u = ∫ v in (0:ℝ)..u, (1 - Real.cos v)

u - sin u = ∫₀^u (1 - cos v) dv.

Claimed by no released node.

one_sub_sinc_ge_of_le_pi lemma source
lemma one_sub_sinc_ge_of_le_pi {u : ℝ} (hu : 0 < u) (hπ : u ≤ Real.pi) :
    2 / (3 * Real.pi ^ 2) * u ^ 2 ≤ 1 - Real.sinc u

The bound below π: 1 - sin u / u ≥ (2/(3π²)) u² for 0 < u ≤ π.

Claimed by no released node.

one_sub_sinc_ge theorem source
theorem one_sub_sinc_ge (u : ℝ) :
    2 / (3 * Real.pi ^ 2) * min 1 (u ^ 2) ≤ 1 - Real.sinc u

The truncation inequality, pointwise: 1 - sin u / u ≥ (2/(3π²)) (1 ∧ u²).

This is the inequality that makes the weighted partition measures (1 ∧ x²) Π̃ₙ have uniformly bounded mass in thm:increments-levy; the tail half of the same step is Mathlib's measureReal_abs_gt_le_integral_charFun.

Claimed by no released node.

Wedge

LieWedge structure source
structure LieWedge (L : Type*) [AddCommGroup L] [Module ℝ L] where

A Lie wedge: a closed cone in a Lie algebra, closed under the bracket only in the weak (semigroup) sense (blueprint: def:lie-wedge). Not available in Mathlib; modelled locally.

The convex-cone content is captured concretely here: the carrier contains 0 and is closed under addition and non-negative real scaling. The remaining two properties of a *Lie* wedge — topological closedness and weak bracket-closure ⁅v,w⁆ ∈ wedge (one-sided) — need a topology and a Lie bracket on L and are deferred; for translation-invariant symbols the bracket vanishes identically (lem:translation-invariant), so weak bracket-closure is automatic there.

Claimed by no released node.

CovariantTensor structure source
structure CovariantTensor (L : Type*) [Ring L] where

Infinitesimal covariance: [A_v, B_w] = B_{C v w} (blueprint: def:infinitesimal-covariance). A is the Lie-algebra action of G, B the semigroup generators, C the covariance tensor.

Claimed by no released node.
